# Contract-Change Ledger — Non-Critical Surfaces

Campaign: audit-only inventory (P1) → cluster-at-a-time remediation (P2) → recurrence ratchet (P3).
Started 2026-08-16. Skill: `contract-change` (global, `~/.claude/skills/contract-change`), MSW-governed (one loop).

## Scope declaration

**In scope (candidate sources):** `packages/{cli,next-plugin,vite-plugin,unplugin,oracle,_assertions,_integration,_parity,test-ds,showcase}`, `packages/extract/{pipeline,session,tests}` (TS side only), `e2e/*`, `scripts/`, `tools/oxlint/anti-slop`, root `vite.config.ts`.

**Excluded (critical / frozen):** `packages/system` (type-system fulcrum + DX), `packages/properties` (type-surface adjacent), `packages/extract/crates/**` (Rust core), `legacy/**` (archived), `openspec/**`, generated/vendored paths.

Owner search is repository-global: excluded surfaces may be cited as existing owners; they are never edited and never produce candidates.

**Baseline instrument:** `scan-abstraction-collisions.mjs --all` @ working tree of 391df7a (dirty, anti-slop campaign uncommitted). Reports: `.git/contract-change/reports/p1-baseline-all.json` (988 candidates; 671 in scope), `p1-inscope-digest.md`.

**Proof baseline (pre-remediation, 2026-08-16):** `vp run verify:compile` exit 0; `vp run verify:unit:ts` exit 0 (147 files, 1721 tests, 14.5s). All P2 iteration proofs measure against this green state. `vp run verify:lint` exit 1 with exactly **408 anti-slop errors** — the known mid-flight residual of the separate anti-slop campaign (849→408, scripts lane deferred), NOT part of this campaign's baseline obligations. P2 lint gate: the error count must never increase; remediations that clear errors (e.g. the two `no-module-mocking` errors) record the delta.

## Row schema

`claim | lane | file:line | existing owner/analog | disposition | evidence | status`

- **lane:** vocabulary / boundary / flow / state / policy / proof
- **disposition:** REUSE / INLINE / REMOVE / PROMOTE / DISTINCT (+ UNRESOLVED only while a named semantic question is open)
- **status:** U (unresolved) → A (audited, disposition assigned) → R (remediated) → V (verified). Phase close requires zero U rows.

## Ledger

Rows assembled programmatically from `.git/contract-change/reports/p1-lane-*.md` (full 8-column originals preserved there; semantic comparison and dependency legality folded into evidence here).

### Vocabulary

Audit-only. No repository file was edited. Worktree state audited: dirty tree at `main` (391df7a) with the uncommitted lint campaign applied (160 changed paths; `tools/` untracked/new).

Instruments used throughout: `grep -rn` / `grep -rc` over `packages/ e2e/ scripts/ tools/` excluding `node_modules` and `dist`; `git diff HEAD` / `git show HEAD:<path>` to separate campaign-introduced from pre-existing declarations; `md5` over `awk`-extracted body ranges for byte-equality claims; targeted `Read`/`sed` of declaration sites and their owners; `grep -rn openspec/` for spec-pinned surfaces.

**Standing note on the campaign.** The anti-slop rules `no-unknown-parameters` / `no-unsafe-dictionary-type` / `no-unknown-type-aliases` (vendored at `tools/oxlint/anti-slop/rules/`) forbid `unknown`-typed parameters and broad dictionaries. The observed effect across the tree is that a single generic helper per module was replaced by a locally-named JSON value type plus a guard family. Net-new in the working tree: 25 `JsonValue`/`JsonObject` declarations and 28 `isJson*` guards (`git diff HEAD -U0 | grep -cE '^\+function isJson…'`). The rules do not require this shape — a shared exported `JsonValue` is not `unknown` and satisfies every rule — so the duplication is a choice the campaign made, not one the linter forced. That distinction is the axis most rows below turn on.

---

### Cluster: JSON value vocabulary (campaign-introduced, largest single claim)

| `MANIFEST_FILE = 'manifest.json'`, `COMMIT_FILE = 'commit.json'` (hardcoded literals) | vocabulary | `packages/oracle/src/host/animus/loader.ts:8,12`                                   | `packages/cli/src/writer.ts:41,42`, which **re-exports the session constants** (`MANIFEST_ARTIFACT`, `CLI_COMMIT_ARTIFACT` from `@animus-ui/extract/session`) rather than spelling them, under the comment: _"a drifted local literal on either side breaks the contract silently"_ (writer.ts:35-38) | **DISTINCT** + tether test — oracle is private with extract as devDep whose ./session entry is built dist (runtime import couples oracle to build:ts; repo precedent `host-layer-parity.test.ts`); pin spellings with a tether test; nested REUSE: collapse `obligations.ts:19 MANIFEST_ORIGIN` onto `loader.ts:8` (see `p1-uresolve-verification.md` V2) | The CLI **writes** these files; the oracle **reads** them (`loadAnimusArtifacts`, `places/snapshot.ts:152 ARTIFACT_FILES`). Same bytes, same contract, two authorities — and the producer's source explicitly warns about exactly the failure mode the consumer instantiates. A third authority exists in `packages/oracle/__tests__/{places-session,places-warm,places-compare}.test.ts`, which hardcode `'manifest.json'` again rather than importing the oracle's own constant. · **dep:** `@animus-ui/extract` is already a devDependency of `packages/oracle` (`packages/oracle/package.json:15`), so importing the session constants is dependency-legal today. ### Coverage

**Covered fully** (every digest row inspected, and each cluster's owner candidate opened and read):

- `packages/cli/src` (8 digest rows) — config guards, exit codes, `writer.ts` artifact constants.
- `packages/_assertions` (4 rows) — full source listing read (`index.ts`, `receipt.ts`, `assert-css.ts`, `assert-appearance.ts`, `find-build-assets.ts`), plus `CLAUDE.md` and `package.json`.
- `packages/_parity/src` and `tools` (20 non-corpus rows) — `engine-run.ts` and `content-hash.ts` read in the relevant ranges; corpus fixtures treated as the fixture family.
- `packages/_integration` (19 rows) — `run-pipeline.ts`, `read-fixtures.ts`, `assert-no-unresolved-tokens.ts`, `manifest-shape.test.ts` declaration blocks.
- `packages/test-ds` (5 rows) — `dev-types.ts` read; the four component rows are the fixture-component family.
- `scripts/` (60 rows) — `hygiene/` and `verify/` file lists enumerated; every duplicated symbol pair opened and compared (with `md5` where byte-equality is claimed).
- `tools/oxlint/anti-slop` (35 rows) — full file listing, `diff -rq` against the upstream skill assets, duplicated helpers located.
- `packages/showcase` (18 rows) — the two type-duplication rows (`Language`, `Param`) traced to their owners; the remaining rows are component-name recurrences inside one app (`Panel`, `Pulse`, `Fade`, `Badge`, `Header`, `ClientPage`) and are the fixture-component family.
- `e2e/*` (~100 rows) — assert-build script duplication traced (`compact`, `assertDir`, `emitLaneReceipt`, `main`, `JsonValue`); fixture apps and their component families summarized as instructed.
- Cross-cutting JSON vocabulary — enumerated exhaustively by grep across the whole tree, with in-scope/out-of-scope counts separated.
- Root `vite.config.ts` — inspected for the anti-slop wiring; contributes **no** candidate rows to the digest (confirmed by filtering the digest on `^vite.config`).

**Covered partially:**

- `packages/oracle` (100 rows). Every guard/type/constant row and the four engine test files were opened. Not individually opened: the ~30 rows in `src/engines/{cascade,diff,explain,format,inspect,refine,simulate,speculate}.ts` and `src/places/*` whose digest reason is only "1 same-name … declaration" against a sibling in the same package. The newly-added untracked `src/providers/render-tree.ts` (which replaces the deleted `render-shape.ts`) was **not** audited for vocabulary claims — it is net-new code and warrants its own pass.
- `packages/next-plugin` (103 rows). All type-duplication and JSON-vocabulary rows opened; the loader/plugin production types read. The test-fixture composer rows (`makeSession`, `startSession`, `runLoader`, `write`) were resolved from the digest's `calls=` and same-name counts plus one or two sampled bodies each, not by reading all 5 sites of each.
- `packages/vite-plugin` (36 rows). `context-probe.ts`, `hot-update-events.ts`, `dev-lane/{fixture,scenario}.ts` and the three JSON-vocabulary test files read. The `dispatch`/`record` recurrences inside `hot-update.test.ts` (three declarations in one file) were not opened.
- `packages/extract` (59 rows). `pipeline/{engine-adapter,source-ingestion,asset-placeholders,assemble-stylesheet,svelte-source-adapter,analyze-project-args}.ts` and `session/{session-paths,singleton,turbopack-orchestrator,extraction-session}.ts` read at the flagged ranges. The ~25 `tests/fixtures/*.tsx` rows are the fixture-component family and were not opened individually.

**Not covered:**

- `packages/system`, `packages/properties`, `packages/extract/crates`, `legacy/` — excluded as candidate sources per the lane brief. `packages/system/__tests__` was cited only as evidence of the JSON vocabulary's true spread (2 more `JsonValue` copies, 3 more `isJson*` guards) and is otherwise untouched.
- Runtime-behaviour verification of any kind. No build, no test run, no `verify` tier — per the audit-only constraint. Every "identical"/"drifted" claim rests on byte comparison (`md5`, `diff`) or on reading both sites, and is labelled as such in the evidence column.
- `openspec/specs/` was grepped for the specific symbols where a REMOVE was in play (`asProbeStateId`, `envelopeCssArtifact`, `getOwningWatchSession`). It was **not** swept exhaustively against every symbol in the report, because no other row recommends REMOVE — the repo prior only binds that disposition.

### Boundary

Audit-only pass over the current (dirty) worktree. No repository file was modified.
Instruments: `rg`, `Read`, `git status`/`git check-ignore`, `.git/contract-change/reports/p1-inscope-digest.md`.
No build, no test, no verify command was run — every semantic claim below is read from source, not observed at runtime.

**Repo-specific frame that governs most dispositions.** `vite.config.ts` (`lint.jsPlugins` → `./tools/oxlint/anti-slop/index.ts`) turns this lane's doctrine into repo-wide `error`-level rules: `no-runtime-typeof` (typeof only inside a type predicate), `no-unknown-parameters`, `no-unknown-returns`, `no-unknown-type-aliases`. Only `packages/system`'s 33-file frozen list and `tools/oxlint/anti-slop/**` are exempt. Consequence: **a local `JsonValue`-domain type plus locally-typed narrowing predicates is the shape the repo's own linter demands at every ingress**, and a shared generic `isRecord(value: unknown)` is the shape it forbids. Several duplications below are therefore the _intended_ output of the doctrine, not slop — and a naive "consolidate the guards" pass would regress it. The genuine findings are elsewhere: duplicated _wire-shape declarations_, duplicated _policy_, and `unknown` that never crossed a process boundary at all.

`tools/` is untracked (`git status` → `?? tools/`), so the anti-slop plugin itself is part of the uncommitted campaign.

### Cluster F — Silent degradation on the pipeline's own internal string channels

Every payload in this cluster is produced by this repo's own code (system loader, plugin config builders), never by a user. A parse failure is therefore an internal invariant violation, and the reference's resolution pattern _"Catch masks an impossible internal state → Remove and allow the invariant violation to surface"_ applies.

| claim                                                                                          | lane     | file:line                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | existing owner/analog                                                                    | disposition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | status |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ |
| A diagnostics collector whose own doc says it "must fail loud" returns `[]` on malformed input | boundary | `packages/extract/pipeline/manifest-diagnostics.ts:94-99`                                                                                                                                                                                  | its own header at `:82-89`: _"The system-config boundary is where these must fail loud"_ | **REMOVE the catch** (or convert to a diagnostic). Fallback gate: protected contract — none nameable; consumer — none; owner — the system-config boundary itself. Defaults to REMOVE                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `JSON.parse(selectorAliasesJson)` failing → `return []` → zero diagnostics → the selector-alias validation silently passes. The failure mode the function exists to prevent is exactly the one the catch creates · **dep:** — · **instrument:** Read `manifest-diagnostics.ts:82-115`                                                                                                        | V      |
| A parse failure is **memoized**, converting a transient failure into a process-lifetime policy | boundary | `packages/extract/pipeline/resolve-asset.ts:45-58 parseAliasTable`                                                                                                                                                                         | —                                                                                        | **REMOVE the catch** (cache only successful parses at minimum). ### Coverage

**Fully covered** (every declaration site read, not sampled):

- The `JsonValue` / `isJsonObject` / `isJsonString` / `isJsonNumber` / `isJsonBoolean` family — all 12 type declarations and all guard bodies across `packages/{_parity,_integration,next-plugin,vite-plugin,system}` and `e2e/svelte-app`.
- Config ingestion for all four drivers: `cli/src/config.ts`, `unplugin/src/options.ts`, `vite-plugin/src/{index.ts,config.ts}`, `next-plugin/src/with-animus.ts`, and the shared owner `extract/pipeline/core-options.ts` in full.
- The `Object.prototype.toString.call` guard family repo-wide (enumerated exhaustively by grep, every production site read).
- Error classes in the in-scope packages (exhaustive grep) and the CLI's exit-code translation path.
- `commit.json`, the `.animus` lock, `analysis-commit`, `analysis-status`, and the replacement-epoch artifact: every reader and writer.
- `scripts/hygiene` oxlint/knip report decoders.

**Partially covered** (representative reads, not exhaustive):

- `packages/extract/pipeline` — I enumerated all 20 `JSON.parse` sites and read the boundary-relevant ones (`core-options`, `run-analysis`, `manifest-diagnostics`, `resolve-asset`, `external-keyframes`, `correlate-external-tokens`, `engine-adapter`, `source-ingestion`, `analyze-project-args`). I did **not** read `prefix.ts`, `tsconfig-paths.ts` (jsonc stripper), or `system-config.ts` closely; each has its own parse-and-cast and may add rows to Cluster F.
- `packages/oracle` — `manifest-types`, `loader`, `replacement`, `universe`, `css-parse`, `selector`, `session`, `render`, `prove`, `args`, `core/identity`, `core/probe` read. `engines/{diff,simulate,inspect,explain,refine,equivalence}` read only where `semanticDiff` flows.
- `packages/next-plugin/src` — `with-animus.ts` and `turbopack-loader.ts` read in full; `plugin.ts`, `loader-core.ts`, `turbopack-config.ts` not audited for boundary decoders.
- `scripts/verify/*` — 12 `JSON.parse … as Manifest/Workflow/PackageManifest` sites identified by grep (`workspace-graph.ts:28`, `packed-graph.ts:197,278`, `owner-graph.test.ts:132`, `workers-config.test.ts:23,33`, `ci-graph.test.ts:65`, `topology.ts:445,459`, `rust-policy.ts:276`, `attw-def5.ts:240`, `v8-to-lcov.mjs:204`). These are almost certainly a `PackageManifest`-shape duplication cluster of the same kind as Cluster D, but I read none of them — **not audited**.

**Uncovered** — stated plainly:

- `packages/showcase`, `packages/test-ds` — not examined at all (no boundary-shaped hits in the guard/JSON greps, but I did not read them).
- `e2e/{packed-app,react-router-app,vinext-app,next16-app}` beyond the `assert-build.ts` heads; `e2e/rollup-app/scripts/assert-watch.mjs` (two `JSON.parse` sites at `:148,:158`) and `assert-host.mjs` not read.
- `packages/extract/tests` (TS) — in scope per the brief, not examined.
- `packages/_parity` beyond `src/engine-run.ts`; `packages/_assertions` beyond `assert-css`, `assert-appearance`, `receipt`, `index` (`assert-html.ts`, `find-build-assets.ts` unread).
- Root `vite.config.ts` was read for lint scoping only, not audited as a boundary surface.
- **No runtime verification of any semantic claim.** All null/array/prototype/boxed-primitive comparisons are reasoned from the source expressions, not executed. The claims most worth executing before acting: `typeof record.payloads !== 'object'` accepting arrays (Cluster C row 1) and `isConfigRecord` accepting `Date`/boxed objects (Cluster B row 5).
- **Spec grep is narrow.** `openspec/specs/` (147 spec dirs) returned zero hits for `isRecord`, `asManifest`, `verifyPublishedSet`, `exitCodeFor`, `AnimusConfigError`, `semanticDiff`, `isJsonObject`, `projectResolvedConfig`; the gate was calibrated non-vacuous against `Requirement`. Only `openspec/changes/standalone-extraction-cli/` was read for mandates. The one **REMOVE** recommendation (`render.ts` guards, Cluster E) and the **REMOVE the catch** recommendations (Cluster F) rest on that check; the other 19 changes under `openspec/changes/` were not grepped.

### Flow

Audit only. No repository file was modified. Worktree state audited: dirty tree at `main` (391df7a) with the uncommitted lint campaign in place.

**Gate applied** (`~/.claude/skills/contract-change/references/boundary-and-fallback-review.md` § Fallback evidence gate): a fallback/compat path may stay ONLY when all four of _protected contract_, _known current consumer or observed failure mode_, _owner_, _removal condition_ are explicit. Absent that evidence the default is REMOVE. Legacy shapes normalize at ONE migration/ingress boundary, never as a permanent second steady-state path.

**Dependency law checked per row** (AGENTS.md § Workspace Topology, enforced by `scripts/verify/topology.ts:111-115`): `e2e/*` MAY import `packages/*`; `packages/*` MUST NOT import `e2e/*` or `legacy/*`; nothing imports `legacy/*`. No row below proposes or discovers an illegal edge.

**Spec prior**: `openspec/specs/` is present in this worktree (60 capability dirs). Every REMOVE row was grepped against it; spec-pinned constructs carry an explicit spec-sync note in Evidence.

### Coverage

**Fully covered**

- Every occurrence of `legacy` / `compat` / `compatibility` / `fallback` / `shim` / `deprecated` / `backcompat` in candidate `.ts`/`.tsx`/`.mjs`/`.js`/`.sh` sources (131 source hits + 59 e2e/scripts/tools hits, both enumerated and read in context).
- Every `retry` / `backoff` / `poll` / `attempt` / `sleep` / `setTimeout` / `setInterval` hit in `packages/{cli,next-plugin,vite-plugin,unplugin,oracle}/src`, `packages/extract/{pipeline,session}`, and `scripts/` (64 hits).
- All four `createV2EngineApi` call sites and all three hand-rolled engine adapters.
- All `typeof … === 'function'` capability probes in scope (5 hits).
- The named surfaces from the brief: `svelte-source-adapter.ts`, `compatibility-barrier.test.ts`, `engine-retirement.test.ts`, next-plugin turbopack-vs-webpack orchestration, `unplugin/src/core.ts`, `extract/session/singleton.ts`, and the `vite.config.ts` task graph.
- `openspec/specs/` grepped for every REMOVE row; spec-sync obligations are stated inline where they exist.

**Partially covered**

- **`catch` blocks**: 148 in candidate source. I read every catch in `cli/src`, `unplugin/src`, `extract/session`, `extract/pipeline`, and the vite/next transform + loader paths — the paths that decide serve-vs-withhold, delete-vs-keep, and analyze-vs-passthrough. Catches inside `packages/oracle/src` render/CLI code and inside `packages/_assertions` were sampled, not exhaustively read.
- **`packages/oracle`**: reviewed for dual readers (`replacement.ts` `parseConfig` JSON-vs-structural recovery at `:219-276` — **DISTINCT**, named cause (group-spread configs emit valid JS/invalid JSON), surfaced to the caller as a `note`, narrows rather than invents; **removal condition missing**). The oracle's engine/tier internals were not swept for flow constructs beyond keyword and retry greps.
- **`packages/showcase` and `packages/test-ds`**: keyword-swept only. Showcase hits were MDX documentation prose about the _system's_ unit-fallback and theme-fallback features, not runtime flow constructs.
- **`e2e/*` assertion scripts**: keyword-swept and the compat-relevant fixtures (`next-app/src/ds.ts`, `react-router-app/src/ds.ts`, `rollup-app/src/ds.ts`) read in full. The `assert-build.ts` bodies were not read end-to-end; their `fallback` hits are all about the _emitted CSS_ system-color-scheme fallback blocks (an emitter feature, not a code-flow compat path).
- **`packages/_assertions`**: its 9 `fallback` hits were confirmed to describe the emitter's guarded system-fallback CSS blocks (spec: `system-color-scheme`), i.e. product semantics, not a flow construct. Not otherwise audited.

**Uncovered**

- `packages/extract/crates/**` (Rust) and `packages/system` / `packages/properties` — excluded as candidates per the brief. Two rows above (`selectorOrderJson`, the `tokens` export-name fallback) terminate in these trees and are recorded as UNRESOLVED/DISTINCT rather than resolved, because their owning code was out of scope to read.
- `packages/oracle` test tiers and `packages/next-plugin/tests/webpack-gauntlet/**` were not audited for compat paths kept alive by harness code, beyond the specific tests named in rows above.
- `tools/oxlint/anti-slop`: keyword sweep returned no flow constructs; the rule implementations themselves were not read.
- Git archaeology (`git log -S` / `git blame`) was used only for `unstable_turbopack` (introduced in `c393a9d`, touched by `c7b9b3d`). Other rows were dated from in-repo evidence (peer ranges, spec text, writer/reader pairing) rather than history.

### State

Audit-only pass over the dirty worktree at `main` (163 changed paths, lint campaign uncommitted). No repository file was modified.

Scope audited: `packages/{cli,next-plugin,vite-plugin,unplugin,oracle,_assertions,_integration,_parity}`, `packages/extract/{pipeline,session,tests}` (TS only), `e2e/*`, `scripts/`, `tools/oxlint/anti-slop`, root `vite.config.ts`. `packages/system`, `packages/properties`, `packages/extract/crates`, `legacy/`, `openspec/` treated as citable owners only.

Lane question applied to every row: **what is the source of truth, who writes each representation, what refreshes/invalidates it, and under what concrete sequence can two representations disagree?**

### Coverage

**Fully covered**

- `packages/extract/session/` — `singleton.ts`, `extraction-session.ts` (field inventory, publication transaction, watch-transaction gating, epoch publication, sibling reconciliation, asset memo, rollback snapshot), `turbopack-orchestrator.ts`, `published-set.ts`, `session-paths.ts` (by reference).
- `packages/next-plugin/src/` — `loader.ts`, `turbopack-loader.ts`, `plugin.ts` epoch/needBuild state, `with-animus.ts` watcher wiring and warn flags.
- `packages/unplugin/src/core.ts` — full `HostState` lifecycle.
- `packages/cli/src/` — `watch.ts`, `writer.ts` lock/commit, `build.ts` session construction (read at the call sites reached from `watch.ts`).
- `packages/oracle/` — `cli/session.ts`, `places/snapshot.ts`, `core/world.ts`, `engines/cascade.ts` memos.
- `packages/_parity/src/register.ts`; `packages/_assertions/src/receipt.ts` and all four emitters; `scripts/hygiene/_receipts.ts`.
- Module-level mutable state across the whole scope: enumerated exhaustively via `^(export )?(const|let) … = new (Map|Set|WeakMap|WeakSet)` and `^let ` sweeps; every production (non-test) hit is dispositioned above.

**Partially covered**

- `packages/vite-plugin/src/` — `context.ts` (1101 lines) read at the state-declaration, publication, geological-reset, asset, and ingestion regions; `hmr.ts` (22 KB) read only at the presentation-only gate, the external-owner branch, and delete pruning; `build-start.ts`, `module-invalidation.ts`, `index-html.ts`, `css.ts` inspected by grep only. The dual-spine claim (Cluster C row 1) is evidenced field-by-field but the per-field behavioral divergences between the two spines are NOT individually verified — only the `externalFileOwners` projection was traced end to end.
- `packages/extract/pipeline/` — audited for module-level mutable state and for the three memos named above (`replacement-plans.ts`, `resolve-asset.ts`, `correlate-external-tokens.ts` `indexCache` was located at `:140` but its key derivation was not read). Other pipeline modules were not opened.
- `packages/cli/src/config.ts`, `packages/cli/src/build.ts` prune/hygiene paths — not read in full.
- Test-side state: only `singleton-fixtures.ts`, `webpack-gauntlet/harness.ts`, `run-pipeline.ts` were opened. The ~40 test files holding `tempRoots`/`restoreGlobals` module arrays follow one visible convention and were not individually audited.

**Uncovered**

- `packages/showcase/` — only `src/pages/Home.tsx:24` (`let revealCounter = 0`) was located; no showcase state was dispositioned.
- `packages/test-ds/` — not examined.
- `e2e/*` beyond the four receipt emitters and the `assert-*.mjs` accumulator arrays surfaced by the module-level sweep.
- `tools/oxlint/anti-slop/` — its module-level `Set`s are all frozen policy vocabularies (`commentOwnerKinds`, `moduleMockMethods`, `functionBoundaryTypes`, `BUILT_INS`, `TRANSPARENT_WRAPPERS`); no mutable rule state was found, and none was dispositioned.
- Root `vite.config.ts` (111 added lines in this diff) — not read.
- `scripts/verify/*` beyond the receipt/lane surface (`topology.ts`, `owner-graph.test.ts`, `packed-graph.ts`, `v8-to-lcov.mjs` accumulator state) — not dispositioned.
- Rust-side state in `packages/extract/crates` (out of scope by instruction) — the `ExtractEngine` retained facts/sources it owns are the counterpart to several TS memos above; the TS↔Rust cache-coherence question is therefore NOT covered by this lane.

### Policy

Audit-only pass over the current (dirty) worktree at `/Users/sugarat/agent-workspaces/me-im-counting/animus`.
Lane definition: defaults, permissions, statuses, filtering, error translation, ordering decisions.
A collision = another location deciding the SAME status/default/permission/filter/ordering/error-translation such that the two could drift.

No repository file was modified. Instruments: `grep` (ugrep via the shell wrapper), `Read`, `ls`. No builds, no verify tiers.

Legend for **dependency legality**: whether the dependency edge the recommended disposition would create is legal under `AGENTS.md` § Workspace Topology / `scripts/verify/topology.ts` (`packages/* ↛ e2e/*|legacy/*`; `e2e/* → packages/*` permitted).

### Coverage

**Fully covered**

- The four plugin-driver option/config entry points: `packages/vite-plugin/src/config.ts`, `packages/next-plugin/src/with-animus.ts`, `packages/unplugin/src/options.ts`, `packages/cli/src/config.ts`, plus the shared core `packages/extract/pipeline/core-options.ts` (read end-to-end).
- Extension / include-exclude / transform-claim policy across all four drivers and the Turbopack rule (Cluster A, B).
- Default values: `outDir`, `mode`, `minify`, `verbose`, `extensions`, `exclude`, root authority (Clusters B, C, D).
- CLI error translation and exit taxonomy: `packages/cli/src/{index,build,watch,writer}.ts` read in full.
- `scripts/verify/*.sh` precondition/`PREPARE:`/exit surface: `_preconditions.sh` read in full, all 17 shell scripts surveyed for `exit`/`recipe`/`require_*`/`PREPARE`.
- Root `vite.config.ts` in full: lint/fmt/test ignore lists, anti-slop severities, `run.tasks` ordering.
- `tools/oxlint/anti-slop` severity/default surface; confirmed absence of any `.oxlintrc*`.
- CSS `@layer` ordering authority and its assertion-lane restatements (Cluster I1/I2), cross-checked against `openspec/specs/layer-declaration-delivery` and `plugin-self-verify`.
- Lane failure/exit conventions across all nine `e2e/*/scripts/assert-*` files and `scripts/assert-showcase-build.ts` (heads and tails read; four full reads).

**Partially covered**

- `packages/extract/session/extraction-session.ts` (~2400 lines): read the scan-config, verbose, warn/log, mode/minify, and watch-classification regions. The watch **debounce / single-flight / batch-coalescing** ordering policy and the geological-reset classification were surveyed by grep only, not read line-by-line. Watcher **registration order** (`startTurbopackWatcher` → external roots → `onDied`) was inspected only from the CLI consumer side (`watch.ts`), not inside `turbopack-orchestrator.ts`.
- `packages/next-plugin/src/plugin.ts` (webpack plugin): root-authority lines and `ANIMUS_CSS_MODULE_ID` uses were read; its multi-compiler ownership / epoch-guard **status derivation** (which compiler is authoritative, when a build is "needed") was not audited — it is a large surface that reads as flow/state lane more than policy.
- `packages/oracle`: only the CLI exit taxonomy and `DEFAULT_ARTIFACT_DIR` were audited. The oracle's **verdict derivation** (`ProbeVerdict` → PROVED/DISPROVED/FIXPOINT/UNSETTLED) is a status-derivation policy that plausibly belongs to this lane and was not compared against `packages/_parity`'s PASS/FAIL scoreboard model beyond exit codes.
- `packages/_parity/src/engine-run.ts` (modified in the dirty tree): only its exit path was read.

**Uncovered**

- `packages/showcase` and `packages/test-ds` — no policy surfaces sampled.
- `packages/_integration` — treated as proof surface; its fixture/status conventions were not audited for policy duplication.
- `packages/extract/tests` (TS side) — not audited; test-local policy restatements (beyond the layer-order and exclude-matcher tests already surfaced) may exist.
- `scripts/build`, `scripts/cloudflare`, `scripts/deploy`, `scripts/release.sh` — not opened. Deploy/release gating is a plausible policy surface (environment decisions, publish preconditions) left unexamined.
- `scripts/hygiene/run.sh` (432 lines) — only its `exit`/mode-validation lines were surveyed; its `SUGGESTED_EXIT` derivation and layer-cascade status model were not audited.
- `.github/workflows/ci.yaml` — inspected only through `ci-graph.test.ts`; the YAML itself was not read, so environment/permission policy inside it is unassessed.
- `e2e/*/next.config.ts`, `vite.config.ts`, and other consumer fixture configs — not audited for driver-option restatements.

**Honest caveats**

- Every "currently identical" judgement is a static read of the worktree, not an executed comparison; no tier was run.
- Two rows (B2, D1, E3, F4, H1, J3) are filed UNRESOLVED rather than guessed. Each carries the exact question a decision-maker must answer.
- `openspec/` was consulted for four capabilities (`plugin-self-verify`, `layer-declaration-delivery`, `next-turbopack-integration`, `arch-css-structural-gates`) and grepped for two more. The remaining ~140 specs were not searched, so some rows marked as un-owned may in fact have a spec owner I did not find.

### Proof

Audit-only pass over the current (dirty) worktree at `391df7a`. No repository file was edited.
Instrument: `rg`/`grep`/`Read`/`git` only — no builds, no verify tiers, no test runs. Every
"can drift" verdict below is a static-reading verdict, not an execution result.

Scope per assignment. Excluded as candidate sources (legal as cited owners): `packages/system`,
`packages/properties`, `packages/extract/crates`, `legacy/`, `openspec/`, `node_modules`, `dist`.

Legality rule applied throughout: `packages/*` MUST NOT import `e2e/*`
(`packages/_assertions/CLAUDE.md` § Consumers; root `CLAUDE.md` § Workspace Topology).
`e2e/* → packages/*` is legal and already practised.

### Coverage

**Fully covered**

- `packages/_assertions` (all 6 src modules read end-to-end) and every `e2e/*/scripts/assert-*` script + `scripts/assert-showcase-build.ts` — the "what does e2e re-implement instead of importing" question is answered exhaustively.
- The three tuple→`EngineOptions` adapters (`engine-adapter.ts`, `_parity/src/engine-run.ts`, `_integration/__tests__/run-pipeline.ts`) and the owner tuple (`analyze-project-args.ts`) — read in full and compared field by field.
- `packages/next-plugin/tests/webpack-gauntlet/harness.ts` (884 L) and `singleton-fixtures.ts` (120 L) — read in full; the canonical-vs-local fixture map for next-plugin is complete.
- `packages/vite-plugin/tests/context-probe.ts` + `index-html-context.ts`, and the production `PluginContext.analyzeIngested` / `publishSourceIngestion` they mirror.
- Both `prerequisites.ts` modules — byte-compared.
- The module-mocking question: rule source, config wiring, override list, and a repo-wide suppression sweep.
- Test-only exports in production source across `extract/{session,pipeline}`, `next-plugin/src`, `vite-plugin/src`, `unplugin/src`, `cli/src`, `oracle/src` (grep for `__*`, `*ForTests`, `*Override`).

**Partially covered**

- **vite-plugin test dir (29 suites).** The six `makeContext` factories and the two shared helper modules were read; the other ~21 suites were characterized by import graph and helper-definition grep, not read line by line. Additional per-file duplication is plausible there.
- **next-plugin test dir (30 suites).** The eight `createProject` definitions, all 16 `vi.hoisted` sites, and the gauntlet harness were read; individual suite bodies were not. In particular `webpack-gauntlet/{backdate-tree,harness-scheduling,real-engine,external-ingestion}.test.ts` were not opened.
- **`packages/oracle/__tests__` (27 files).** Verified it does NOT drive the extraction engine (it reads committed `.animus` artifacts: `fixtures/rollup-app/{commit,manifest,styles}`), so the hypothesized `oracle` ↔ `_parity/engine-run` duplication is **refuted**. Small helper repetition (`asTargetId('src/Alert.tsx::Alert')` in 3 core-\* suites, per-file `evidence`/`geometry`/`factInput` builders) was observed but not evaluated in depth — it is fixture-literal repetition, not behaviour mirroring.
- **`packages/_parity` baseline/refresh procedure.** `engine-run.ts` was read in full; `baseline.ts`, `compare.ts`, `register.ts`, `scoreboard.ts` and `refresh-parity-baseline.sh` were NOT read. The assignment's "oracle/baseline files whose refresh procedure can silently accept regressions" question is therefore **only partially answered** — repo memory already records the shape of that risk (`never-baseline-newly-loud-failures`, `parity-refresh-register-dance`), and the `HARNESS_*` constants at `engine-run.ts:213-256` are a live example of harness-supplied inputs whose absence would make baselines green-by-vacuity (the file guards this deliberately with a corpus floor at `:390`). A dedicated read of the refresh path is the top follow-up.
- **`packages/_integration`** — `run-pipeline.ts`, `fixtures/setup.ts`, `fixtures/read-fixtures.ts` read; the 16 test suites were not.

**Uncovered**

- `packages/cli/tests`, `packages/unplugin/tests`, `packages/showcase`, `packages/test-ds` internals, and `packages/extract/tests/*` beyond the `makeRoot`/`tempRoots` grep. `packages/cli/tests/cli-unit.test.ts:36` declares its own `makeRoot` (5th sibling of that name) but was not opened.
- `e2e/rollup-app/scripts/assert-host.mjs` and `assert-watch.mjs` bodies (only their `check`/`failures` vocabulary and import lists were read); `e2e/rollup-app/prototype/measure.mjs`.
- `packages/vite-plugin/tests/dev-lane/{dev-server.test.ts (841 L), fixture.ts, vite-adapter.ts}` — the bundler-neutral `scenario.ts` contract was read (and looks like a deliberately-designed seam, not duplication), but the vite adapter was not compared against the webpack gauntlet's session driver. **The specific assignment question "same driver-session setup duplicated across plugin packages?" is therefore answered only for the prerequisite probes (Cluster F: yes, duplicated) and NOT for the session drivers themselves.** `runWatchSession` (`harness.ts:673`) and `dev-lane/vite-adapter.ts` are the two candidates; both drive `packages/extract/session`, both own a bounded-wait/settle policy. A focused comparison of those two files is the second follow-up.
- Whether any of the above actually executes as described — no tests, lint, or builds were run, per the audit-only constraint. Every "silently green" and "vacuous" verdict in this report is a static-reading hypothesis, and the two flagged UNRESOLVED rows (Cluster C) name the exact executions that would settle them.
